Applying oil undoubtedly does not invalidate the fast. Unavoidable bleeding—such as non-menstrual vaginal bleeding, bleeding from wounds, nosebleeds, and the like—does not invalidate the fast. Menstrual and postpartum bleeding, however, invalidate the fast by scholarly consensus.

As for cupping, there are two well-known opinions. The position of Ahmad and many of the early scholars is that it invalidates the fast. There are two opinions within his school concerning bloodletting and similar procedures; one treats them as equivalent to cupping. His position regarding kohl that reaches the brain is that it invalidates the fast, like perfume, and when needed (1). Mālik holds a similar view. As for Abū Hanīfa and al-Shāfiʿī—may Allah have mercy on them both—they do not consider these things to invalidate the fast. Allah knows best.

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) Thus in the original.

---

## Page 12716

He was asked:

A man underwent bloodletting because of a headache while fasting. Does this invalidate his fast and require him to make up that day, or not? If he is informed that undergoing bloodletting invalidates his fast, is he sinful for doing so, or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. This issue is disputed within Ahmad’s school and elsewhere. The more cautious course is for him to make up that day. Allah knows best.

He was asked:

Does bloodletting during the month of Ramadan invalidate the fast, or not?

He replied:

If he can postpone bloodletting, he should do so. If he needs it because of an illness, he may undergo it, and he must make up the fast according to one of the two scholarly opinions. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 12717

He was asked:

Ramadan came upon a man during his illness, and he was unable to fast. He died owing the fasts of Ramadan, as well as the prayers missed during his illness, while both his parents were still alive. Are these obligations discharged if his parents fast and pray on his behalf, whether or not he left instructions for this?

He replied:

If his illness persisted and he was unable to make up the missed fasts, his heirs are required only to provide food on his behalf. As for the obligatory prayers, no one may perform them on another’s behalf. However, if either parent performs voluntary prayers on behalf of the deceased and dedicates their reward to him, or observes voluntary fasts on his behalf and dedicates their reward to him, that benefits him. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 12718

Moderation in Acts of Worship

The esteemed scholars—may Allah be pleased with them—are kindly requested to resolve the following confusion, which has caused evident harm to worshippers:

One of them heard the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

“The prayer most beloved to Allah is the prayer of David, and the fasting most beloved to Allah is the fasting of David. He would sleep half the night, spend a third of it in prayer, and sleep the remaining sixth. He would fast one day and not fast the next.”

He therefore made a commitment to Allah to fast one day and not fast the next, and continued doing so for a year or more. He is married, has dependants, and works for a living, which requires him to maintain his health. He then developed an ambition to memorize the Quran, so alongside this strenuous regimen he began learning portions each day and repeating them.

In addition, he then became intent on pursuing his spiritual goal, spending most of the night in prayer, and striving and persevering extensively in worship. Thus, he suffered the combined burden of the dryness caused by fasting, diminished strength for his work, the dryness caused by frequent repetition, and the dryness arising from his intense resolve. He is also a young man with the heat of youth. All of this together impaired his mind, causing absent-mindedness, headaches, and dullness

---

## Page 12719

in his understanding, so that he cannot fully grasp the meaning of what he hears. The effects of dryness have also become apparent in his eyes, to the point that they have almost sunk into their sockets.

Through these strenuous efforts, he has experienced some spiritual illumination. He refuses to give up this fasting because of the commitment he made to Allah Most High and his fear of losing the light he has. When someone with spiritual understanding cautions him against this, he offers excuses, saying, “I want to kill myself for Allah’s sake.”

Is his fasting in this condition pleasing to Allah Most High, or is it disliked and displeasing to Him? Is this commitment permissible for him, and is he required to offer expiation for an oath in connection with it, or not? Is attending to his bodily health and preserving his brain, reason, and understanding—so that he can devote himself fully to observing his obligations and caring for his dependants—what Allah is pleased with and desires of him, or not? Does his persistence in this incur Allah Most High’s abhorrence, since he is casting himself into destruction through something that was not obligatory upon him?

Even if this practice is sanctioned by the Sunnah, is it sanctioned unconditionally for everyone, or only for those whom it does not harm?

He asks for this question to be clarified and resolved. This man has baffled physicians and grieved sensible people by embarking upon the spiritual path in ignorance, heedless of what his Lord desires of him. We ask that the answer be put in writing and supported by the Quran and Sunnah, so that it may reach his heart. May Allah Most High reward you and grant the Muslims the benefit of your long lives. May Allah bless our master Muhammad and grant him peace, and may Allah be pleased with all his Companions.

---

## Page 12720

Shaykh al-Islām, the eminent scholar, master of hadith, independent jurist, and mufti to the people, Taqī al-Dīn Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah, replied in his own handwriting:

Praise be to Allah.

The answer to this question rests on two principles: first, what Islamic law requires; and second, what the pledge and vows entail.

As for the first, what is prescribed and commanded, and what Allah and His Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—love, is moderation in worship. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Follow a moderate course; follow a moderate course.”

He also said:

“This religion is firm. No one overburdens himself in religion without being overcome by it. So seek help by travelling in the early morning, the late afternoon, and for part of the night. Be moderate, be moderate, and you will reach your destination.”

Both reports are in al-Sahīh.

Ubayy ibn Ka'b said:

“Moderation in following the Sunnah is better than strenuous effort in following innovation.”

Whenever an act of worship causes a person harm that prevents him from fulfilling an obligation more beneficial to him than that act, it is forbidden. Examples include fasting in a way that leaves him too weak to undertake the earning required of him, impairs the reasoning or understanding required of him, or prevents him from undertaking obligatory jihad.

---

## Page 12721

and likewise if it leads him into something forbidden whose harm cannot be counterbalanced by the benefit of that worship, such as giving away all his wealth and then looking longingly at other people’s wealth and begging from them. If, however, it weakens him for something more beneficial than itself and causes him to fall into disliked acts, then it is disliked.

Concerning this, Allah Most High revealed His words:

“O you who believe, do not forbid the good things Allah has made lawful for you, and do not transgress. Indeed, Allah does not love transgressors.”

This verse was revealed concerning some of the Companions who had gathered and resolved to devote themselves exclusively to worship: one would fast continuously, another would pray throughout the night, another would abstain from eating meat, and another would abstain from women. Allah, glorified and exalted is He, forbade them to prohibit such good things as eating meat and marital relations, and forbade them to transgress. Transgression means going beyond the prescribed religion in fasting, night prayer, recitation, remembrance of Allah, and the like; adding prohibitions beyond what has been forbidden; and extending the bounds of the permissible beyond what has been allowed. He then commanded them to expiate the binding oaths they had sworn to uphold these prohibitions and transgressions.

In the two Sahīh collections, Anas reported:

“A group of the Prophet’s Companions asked the wives of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about his worship in private. One of them said, ‘As for me, I will fast and never break my fast.’ Another said, ‘As for me, I will spend the night in prayer and not sleep.’ Another said, ‘As for me, I will not marry women.’ Another said, ‘As for me,

---

## Page 12722

I will not eat meat.’

Word of this reached the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and he said, ‘What is the matter with people who say such-and-such? Yet I pray and sleep, fast and break my fast, marry women, and eat meat. Whoever turns away from my Sunnah is not of me.’”

The authentic hadith collections also report through several chains:

“‘Abdullāh ibn ‘Amr ibn al-‘Ās had taken to fasting by day, praying by night, and reciting the entire Quran every three days. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade him to do this, saying, ‘Do not do so, for if you do, your eyes will become sunken and your soul will grow weary.’”

That is, the eyes become sunken, and the soul grows weary and bored.

He also told him:

“Your own self has a right over you, your wife has a right over you, and your visitors have a right over you. So give everyone their due.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, thus explained that you have obligations towards yourself, your family, and your visitors. You may not do anything that prevents you from fulfilling these obligatory rights; rather, give everyone their due. Then:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed him to fast three days each month, saying, ‘That is equivalent to fasting every day.’ He also instructed him to recite the Quran once each month. He replied, ‘I am capable of more than that.’ He kept asking to do more until the Prophet said, ‘Then fast one day and refrain from fasting the next, for that is the best way of fasting.’ He replied, ‘I am capable of more

---

## Page 12723

than that.’ The Prophet replied, ‘There is nothing better than that.’”

When ‘Abdullāh ibn ‘Amr grew old, he used to say, “If only I had accepted the concession granted by the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace!” Sometimes he was unable to fast on alternate days, so he would refrain from fasting for several days, then fast for an equal number of consecutive days. This was to avoid changing the practice he had maintained when he parted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

This was because his body could bear it. For some people, however, fasting on alternate days distracts them from something better, so fasting is not the best course for them. Such was the case with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whose practice was better than David’s fast. Nevertheless, it is authentically reported that:

“He was asked about someone who fasts every day, and he said, ‘Whoever fasts every day has neither fasted nor refrained from fasting.’”

“He was asked about someone who fasts for two days and refrains from fasting for one, and he said, ‘Who can manage that?’”

“He was asked about someone who fasts for one day and refrains from fasting for two, and he said, ‘I wish I had been given the strength for that.’”

“He was asked about someone who fasts on alternate days, and he said, ‘That is the best way of fasting.’”

He thus stated that he wished he had the strength to fast one-third of the time. This was because he had duties that were more binding upon him and more beloved to Allah, alongside which he could not manage to fast one-third of the time. It is likewise authentically reported that:

“When he drew near the enemy during the campaign of the Conquest

---

## Page 12724

in Ramadan, he ordered his Companions to break their fast. When word reached him that some people had continued fasting, he said, ‘Those are the disobedient ones.’”

“On one occasion, he prayed while mounted and instructed those with him to pray on their mounts. A man leapt down from his mount and prayed on the ground. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘He has opposed the command; may Allah turn him away.’ Before his death, that man apostatized from Islam.”

Ibn Masʿūd said, “When I fast, I become too weak to recite the Quran, and reciting the Quran is dearer to me.” This is a broad subject that has been discussed at length elsewhere.

As for the “second principle”—concerning someone who pledges to Allah to do this and makes a vow to that effect—its basis is the report recorded in the two Sahīhs from ʿĀʾishah, who said that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever vows to obey Allah, let him obey Him; and whoever vows to disobey Allah, let him not disobey Him.”

If the act that a person has vowed and pledged to Allah involves impermissible harm leading to the neglect of an obligation or the commission of something forbidden, then it is an act of disobedience, and fulfilling the vow is not obligatory. Indeed, even if someone vows to perform a disliked act of worship, such as praying throughout the night and fasting every day, fulfilling that vow is not obligatory.

The scholars then differed over whether such a person must offer the expiation for an oath. There are two views. The stronger view is that he must offer the expiation for an oath, on the basis of what is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace

---

## Page 12725

in the Sahīh, in which he said:

“The expiation for a vow is the expiation for an oath.”

He also said, “A vow is an oath.” And it is reported from him in the Sunan:

“A vow involving disobedience is not binding, and its expiation is the expiation for an oath.”

We have already mentioned the occasion of the verse’s revelation. A similar example is what al-Bukhārī narrated in his Sahīh from Ibn ‘Abbās:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw a man standing in the sun and asked, ‘What is this?’ They replied, ‘This is Abū Isrā’īl. He has vowed to remain standing, not to seek shade or speak, and to fast.’ He said, ‘Tell him to speak, seek shade, sit down, and complete his fast.’”

Since he had vowed to perform acts of worship that were not prescribed—remaining silent, standing, and exposing himself to the sun—the Prophet commanded him to do what was prescribed, namely fasting in his case, and forbade him to do what was not prescribed.

But if someone is unable to perform what he has vowed, or doing so entails hardship, he should offer expiation and perform a substitute for what he vowed. This is illustrated by the hadith of ‘Uqbah ibn ‘Āmir, whose sister vowed to perform Hajj on foot. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah has no need for your sister to torment herself. Tell her to ride and offer a sacrificial animal”—another narration reads, “and fast.”

Thus, this man who pledged to Allah Most High to fast half the time, when doing so has harmed his mind and body, must refrain from fasting, take what will restore his mental and physical health, and offer the expiation for an oath. He should refrain from fasting for as long as is needed to restore his mental and physical health

---

## Page 12726

according to what his condition allows: he may need to refrain from fasting for two-thirds of the time, three-quarters of it, or all of it. Once he has restored his health, he may return to fasting on alternate days if he can do so without harm. Otherwise, he should fast only as much as benefits him and does not distract him from what Allah loves more than fasting. Allah does not love that what is more beloved to Him should be abandoned for something lesser. How, then, could He make that obligatory?

As for the light he experienced through this fasting:

It is well known that acts of worship, as a category, are not wholly evil. Rather, prohibited forms of worship contain both benefit and harm, but because their harm outweighs their benefit, the Lawgiver has forbidden them. Thus, He has forbidden fasting every day, habitually spending the entire night in prayer, and praying after the dawn and afternoon prayers. Yet people experience a light through sustained fasting because of their intense hunger. This is of the same kind as what unbelievers experience, whether among the People of the Book or those without a scripture, such as monks and worshippers of graves. However, this excessive hunger, which goes beyond the prescribed limits, ultimately harms them in this world and the Hereafter, so that the sin it entails outweighs its benefit.

Indeed, we have seen many such people whose excess in the arduous practices they undertake has led them to neglect, discouragement, weariness, and idleness. They may even become entirely cut off from Allah, or be diverted by less meritorious deeds from more meritorious ones, or lose their reason altogether or suffer some impairment of it. This is because their deeds are not founded upon uprightness and adherence to the prophetic example.

---

## Page 12727

As for his statement, “I want to kill myself for Allah’s sake,” this requires clarification. If he does what Allah has commanded him to do and this leads to his death, then he has acted well. An example is someone who charges the enemy ranks alone in a way that benefits the Muslims, believing that he will be killed. This is good.

Concerning such a person, Allah revealed His words:

“And among people is he who sells himself, seeking Allah’s pleasure. And Allah is Most Kind to His servants.”

Another example is how some of the Companions would plunge into the enemy ranks in the presence of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Al-Khallāl narrated through his chain of transmission from ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb:

“A man charged the enemy alone, and the people said, ‘He has cast himself into destruction.’ ‘Umar replied, ‘No; rather, he is among those of whom Allah says: “And among people is he who sells himself, seeking Allah’s pleasure. And Allah is Most Kind to His servants.”’”

But if someone does what he has not been commanded to do, thereby causing his own death, then he is acting wrongfully and transgressing. An example is someone who bathes to remove major ritual impurity in bitter cold, using cold water that he believes will most likely kill him, or who fasts during Ramadan in a way that leads to his death. This is not permissible—how much less so outside Ramadan!

Abū Dāwūd narrated in his Sunan the account of “a man who suffered an injury and asked those with him for a ruling: ‘Do you find any concession allowing me to perform dry ablution?’ They replied, ‘We find no concession for you.’ So he performed a full ritual bath and died. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

---

## Page 12728

‘They killed him—may Allah kill them! Why did they not ask when they did not know? The cure for ignorance is simply to ask.’”

Likewise, the following account is narrated: “When ‘Amr ibn al-‘Ās entered a state of major ritual impurity during the expedition of Dhāt al-Salāsil, it was a cold night, so he performed dry ablution and led his companions in prayer with that ablution. When they returned, they mentioned this to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He asked, ‘O ‘Amr, did you lead your companions in prayer while in a state of major ritual impurity?’

“He replied, ‘O Messenger of Allah, I heard Allah say: “And do not kill yourselves.”’

“He laughed and said nothing.”

Thus, ‘Amr explained that an act of worship leading to one’s death, without serving any benefit that one is commanded to pursue, constitutes the prohibited act of killing oneself. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, approved his understanding.

Killing oneself is forbidden by the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus. As established in the authentic hadith collections, he said:

“Whoever kills himself with something will be punished with it on the Day of Resurrection.”

In another hadith:

“My servant has forestalled Me by taking his own life, so I have forbidden Paradise to him and made Hell his due.”

There is also the hadith about the killer who took his own life when his wounds became severe. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had been saying that he was among the people of Hell—perhaps on account of his evil end. He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would not offer the funeral prayer for anyone who

---

## Page 12729

killed himself;

For this reason, when Samurah ibn Jundab was told that his son had become ill from overeating, he said of him: “Had he died, I would not have offered the funeral prayer for him.”

The believer should therefore distinguish between what Allah has forbidden—intentionally killing oneself or bringing about one’s own death—and what Allah has prescribed: believers selling their lives and wealth to Him. As the Most High says:

“Indeed, Allah has purchased from the believers their lives and their wealth in exchange for Paradise.”

He also says:

“And among the people is he who sells himself, seeking Allah’s pleasure.”

That is, he sells his own self. The criterion in this is what the Quran and Sunnah teach, not what a person finds appealing, feels, or considers right among matters that contradict the Quran and Sunnah. Rather, such a person may fit the description given by ‘Umar ibn ‘Abd al-‘Azīz:

“Whoever worships Allah in ignorance causes more harm than good.”

It should also be understood that Allah’s pleasure and love do not lie in merely tormenting oneself and imposing hardships upon oneself, such that the harder a deed is, the better it must be. Many ignorant people suppose that, in every matter, reward is proportional to hardship. No; rather, reward is proportional to the benefit a deed brings, the good it serves, and its usefulness,

---

## Page 12730

and to the degree of obedience to the commands of Allah and His Messenger. Whichever of two deeds is better in itself, and whose doer is more obedient and more faithful in following, is superior. Deeds do not surpass one another in merit through quantity; rather, their relative merit depends on what is in the hearts while they are performed. Thus,

“When the sister of ‘Uqbah ibn ‘Āmir vowed to perform Hajj barefoot and on foot, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: ‘Allah has no need of your sister tormenting herself. Tell her to ride.’”

It is also reported that “he instructed her to offer a sacrificial animal,” and, in another report, “to fast.”

The same applies to the hadith of Juwayriyah, who was glorifying Allah while counting with pebbles or date stones. He visited her in the forenoon, then returned in the late afternoon and found her still doing the same. He said to her:

“After leaving you, I said four phrases three times. Had they been weighed against what you have said today, they would have outweighed it.”

The foundation of this is for the servant to know that Allah has commanded us only to do what brings us good and has forbidden us only what brings us harm. Thus, Allah praises righteous deeds, commands righteousness and reform, and forbids corruption. Allah, glorified be He, has only forbidden us foul things because of the harm and corruption they entail, and has commanded us to perform righteous deeds because of the benefit and good they bring us. Yet these deeds may only be accomplished

---

## Page 12731

through hardship, as in jihad, Hajj, enjoining what is right, forbidding what is wrong, and seeking knowledge. A person therefore bears that hardship and is rewarded for it because of the benefit that follows. As the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said to ‘Ā’ishah when she performed ‘umrah from al-Tan‘īm in the year of the Farewell Pilgrimage:

“Your reward is proportionate to your exertion.”

But when the benefit of an action does not measure up to its hardship, this is corruption, and Allah does not love corruption. Worldly benefits illustrate this: someone who endures hardship to secure a large profit or repel a formidable enemy acts commendably. But someone who takes on great burdens and severe hardships to obtain a little money or avert a little harm is like someone given a thousand dirhams who exchanges them for a hundred, or someone who travels a day’s journey for a morning meal when he could have a better one in his own town.

Everything prescribed by Islamic law and the Sunnah rests upon justice, balance, and moderation—the best and highest course. This is like al-Firdaws, which is the highest and most central part of Paradise. Whoever follows this course will reach it, if Allah Most High wills.

This applies to every act of worship that is not sought for its own sake, such as hunger, staying awake, and walking. As for what is sought for its own sake, such as knowing Allah, loving Him, and turning to Him

---

## Page 12732

and relying upon Him—perfection in these is prescribed. Yet excess and transgression may occur when things that do not belong to them are introduced into them, such as making the abandonment of means one is commanded to employ part of reliance upon Allah, or making the treatment of forbidden things as lawful and the abandonment of prescribed acts part of love for Him. This, then, is how the matter stands. And Allah, glorified and exalted be He, knows best.

He was asked—may Allah be pleased with him and grant him His good pleasure—about the Night of Decree while he was imprisoned in the Citadel—the Mountain Citadel—in 706 AH.

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah.

The Night of Decree occurs during the last ten nights of Ramadan. It is authentically reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“It is in the last ten nights of Ramadan.”

It falls on an odd-numbered night among them. However, the odd-numbered nights may be reckoned by counting from the beginning of the month. Thus, it should be sought on the twenty-first, twenty-third, twenty-fifth, twenty-seventh, and twenty-ninth nights.

---

## Page 12733

and odd-numbered nights may also be reckoned by counting the nights remaining, as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“When nine nights remain, when seven remain, when five remain, and when three remain.”

Accordingly, if the month has thirty days, these will be the even-numbered nights: the twenty-second night will be the ninth night remaining, and the twenty-fourth will be the seventh night remaining. This is how Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī explained it in the authentic hadith, and this was how the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, spent that month. If the month has twenty-nine days, reckoning by the nights remaining will coincide with reckoning by the nights elapsed.

Since this is so, the believer should seek it throughout all the last ten nights, as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Seek it during the last ten nights.”

It occurs more often during the last seven nights, and most frequently on the twenty-seventh night. Ubayy ibn Ka‘b used to swear that it was the twenty-seventh night. He was asked, “How did you know that?” He replied: “By the sign that the Messenger of Allah told us of. He told us that the sun rises on its morning like a basin, without rays.”

Thus, this sign, which Ubayy ibn Ka‘b reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him,

---

## Page 12734

is among the best-known signs mentioned in hadith. It has also been reported among its signs that “it is a bright, luminous night.” It is calm, neither intensely hot nor intensely cold. Allah may reveal it to some people in a dream or while awake: a person may see its lights, or see someone telling him, “This is the Night of Decree.” His heart may also be opened to a direct perception through which the matter becomes clear to him. And Allah Most High knows best.

He was asked:

“Which is more virtuous: the Night of Decree or the night on which the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, made the Night Journey?”

He answered:

The night of the Night Journey is more virtuous for the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, whereas the Night of Decree is more virtuous for the Muslim community. The special share granted to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, on the night of the Ascension was more complete than his share in the Nights of Decree. The community’s share in the Night of Decree is more complete than their share in the night of the Ascension, although they have an exceedingly great share in that night too. Yet the distinction, honor, and highest rank conferred that night were granted specifically to the one who was taken on the Night Journey, peace and blessings be upon him.

---

## Page 12735

He was also asked: (*)

“Which are more virtuous: the first ten days of Dhū al-Hijjah or the last ten days of Ramadan?”

He answered:

The first ten days of Dhū al-Hijjah are more virtuous than the last ten days of Ramadan, while the last ten nights of Ramadan are more virtuous than the first ten nights of Dhū al-Hijjah.

Ibn al-Qayyim said:

“When a learned and discerning person reflects on this answer, he will find it fully satisfying and sufficient. There are no days during which deeds are more beloved to Allah than the first ten days of Dhū al-Hijjah. They include the Day of ‘Arafah, the Day of Sacrifice, and the Day of Tarwiyah. As for the last ten nights of Ramadan, these are the nights of vigil that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, would spend entirely in worship. Among them is a night better than a thousand months. Whoever answers without making this distinction cannot put forward a sound proof.”

> **Footnotes:**
> Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 203):
> Ibn al-Qayyim, may Allah have mercy on him, recorded this question, answer, and commentary in Badā’i‘ al-Fawā’id, 3:162.

---

## Page 12736

Shaykh al-Islām was asked:

“Which is more virtuous: the Day of ‘Arafah, Friday, the day of Eid al-Fitr, or the Day of Sacrifice?”

He answered:

All praise is due to Allah. The most virtuous day of the week is Friday, by scholarly agreement. The most virtuous day of the year is the Day of Sacrifice. Some scholars have said that it is the Day of ‘Arafah, but the first view is correct, because the Sunan collections report that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The most virtuous day in Allah’s sight is the Day of Sacrifice, followed by the Day of Rest.”

This is also because the Day of Sacrifice is the day of the greater pilgrimage according to Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, and Ahmad, as it is authentically reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The Day of Sacrifice is the day of the greater pilgrimage.”

It includes rites that are not performed on other days, such as the stay at Muzdalifah, stoning Jamrat al-‘Aqabah alone, slaughtering the sacrificial animals, shaving the head, and performing the pilgrimage circumambulation. Performing these rites on this day is preferable, according to the Sunnah and scholarly agreement. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 12737

He was asked: (*)

“Which is more virtuous: Friday or the Day of Sacrifice?”

He answered:

Friday is the most virtuous day of the week, and the Day of Sacrifice is the most virtuous day of the year.

Ibn al-Qayyim said:

Anyone who gives a different answer faces an objection he has no way of refuting.

He was asked:

“Which is the most virtuous day?”

He answered:

All praise is due to Allah. The most virtuous day of the week is Friday: on it Adam was created, admitted into Paradise, and expelled from it. The most virtuous day of the year is the Day of Sacrifice, as it is reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The most virtuous day in Allah’s sight is the Day of Sacrifice, followed by the Day of Rest.”

He was asked:

“A man vowed to fast on Mondays and Thursdays, then decided to fast on alternate days. He could only arrange this by fasting four days and not fasting three, or not fasting four and fasting three. Which is more virtuous? Please give us a ruling; may Allah have mercy on you.”

He answered:

All praise is due to Allah. If he switches from fasting on Mondays and Thursdays to fasting

> **Footnotes:**
> Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd states (p. 204):
> Ibn al-Qayyim also mentions this question, answer, and comment in al-Badā’iʿ, 3:162.

---

## Page 12738

one day and not fasting the next, he has moved to something more virtuous. There is disagreement over this, but the more apparent view is that it is permissible. This is like someone who vows to pray in a less virtuous mosque but prays in a more virtuous one—for example, someone who vows to pray in al-Aqsā Mosque but prays in one of the two Sacred Mosques. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“What has been reported concerning the reward for fasting the three months? And what do you say about observing a devotional retreat and remaining silent during them? Are these righteous deeds or not?”

He answered:

Nothing has been reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, his Companions, or the leading scholars of the Muslims concerning singling out both Rajab and Sha‘bān together for fasting or devotional retreat. Rather, it is established in an authentic report that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, used to fast until Sha‘bān, and at no time of the year would he fast more than he did in Sha‘bān, on account of the month of Ramadan.

As for fasting Rajab specifically, all the hadiths concerning it are weak—indeed, fabricated. Scholars rely on none of them, and they are not among the weak reports narrated concerning

---

## Page 12739

virtuous deeds. Rather, the vast majority are fabricated lies. The most that has been reported in this regard is “that when Rajab began, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, would say, ‘O Allah, bless Rajab and Sha‘bān for us, and let us reach Ramadan.’”

Ibn Mājah narrated in his Sunan, from Ibn ‘Abbās, “that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, forbade fasting Rajab.” Its chain of transmission is open to question. However, it is authentically established that ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb would strike people’s hands to make them partake of food during Rajab, saying, “Do not make it like Ramadan.”

Abū Bakr once entered and saw that his family had bought water jugs and prepared to fast. He asked, “What is this?” They replied, “Rajab.” He said, “Do you wish to make it like Ramadan?” And he broke those jugs.

Thus, if someone refrains from fasting on some days, fasting on the remaining days is not disliked.

In the Musnad and elsewhere, there is a hadith stating that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, instructed people to fast during the sacred months: Rajab, Dhū al-Qa‘dah, Dhū al-Hijjah, and al-Muharram. This concerns fasting during all four months, not singling out Rajab.

As for singling them out for devotional retreat, I know of no instruction to do so. Rather, anyone who observes a fast

---

## Page 12740

that is prescribed and wishes to undertake a devotional retreat while fasting may undoubtedly do so. As for observing a devotional retreat without fasting, there are two well-known views, both transmitted from Ahmad:

The first is that a devotional retreat is not valid without fasting, as is the position of Abū Hanīfah and Mālik.

The second is that a devotional retreat is valid without fasting, as is the position of al-Shāfi‘ī.

As for abstaining from speech altogether, whether during fasting, devotional retreat, or otherwise, this is a blameworthy innovation by scholarly consensus. Whether it is forbidden or merely disliked, however, is a matter on which there are two views within his school and others.

Sahīh al-Bukhārī relates that Abū Bakr al-Siddīq visited a woman from Ahmas and found her observing silence, not speaking. Abū Bakr told her, “This is not lawful; this is a practice of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance.”

Sahīh al-Bukhārī also relates from Ibn ‘Abbās that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, saw a man standing in the sun and asked, “Who is this?” They replied, “This is Abū Isrā’īl. He has vowed to stand in the sun, not to seek shade or speak, and to fast.” He said, “Tell him to sit down, seek shade, speak, and complete his fast.”

Thus, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, commanded him to speak despite his vow of silence, just as he commanded him, despite

---

## Page 12741

his vow to remain standing, to sit down, and, despite his vow not to seek shade, to seek it. He commanded him to fulfil only his vow to fast. This clearly shows that these acts are not means of drawing near to Allah which a person who vows to perform them is commanded to undertake.

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said in an authentic hadith:

“Whoever vows to obey Allah, let him obey Him; and whoever vows to disobey Allah, let him not disobey Him.”

Likewise, a person who vows to perform these acts is not commanded to do so. Whoever performs them as acts of worship and means of drawing near to Allah, adopting them as a religious practice and a path to Allah Most High, is misguided and ignorant, acting contrary to the command of Allah and His Messenger. It is clear that anyone who does this, having vowed a devotional retreat or the like, does so as a religious observance. There is no doubt that doing so as a religious observance is forbidden, for he regards something that is not a means of drawing near to Allah as such, and seeks to draw near to Allah Most High through something Allah does not love. This is forbidden. However, someone who does this before knowledge reaches him may be excused for his ignorance if the proof has not been established against him. Once knowledge reaches him, he must repent.

The guiding principle concerning speech is summed up in the words of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him:

“Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day, let him speak good or remain silent.”

Speaking good—that which is obligatory or recommended—is better than remaining silent about it. As for that which is neither obligatory nor recommended, remaining silent about it is better than saying it.

---

## Page 12742

For this reason, one of the early generations said to his companion, “Remaining silent about evil is better than speaking it.” The other replied, “Speaking good is better than remaining silent about it.”

Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, when you converse privately, do not converse in sin, aggression, and disobedience to the Messenger, but converse in righteousness and piety.”

He also says:

“There is no good in much of their private conversation, except for those who enjoin charity, kindness, or reconciliation between people. Whoever does this seeking Allah’s pleasure, We shall grant him a great reward.”

It is reported in the Sunan collections that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“All the speech of the son of Adam counts against him, not for him, except enjoining what is right, forbidding what is wrong, or remembering Allah Most High.”

There are many hadiths concerning the virtues of silence, and likewise concerning the virtues of speaking good. Remaining silent when speech is obligatory—such as enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong—is forbidden, whether or not one adopts such silence as a religious observance. You must therefore love what Allah and His Messenger love, hate what Allah and His Messenger hate, regard as permissible what Allah and His Messenger have permitted, and regard as forbidden what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden.

---

## Page 12743

He—may Allah have mercy on him—also said:

Section

‘Ā’ishah’s statement,

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, continued to observe devotional retreat during the last ten days until Allah took him in death,”

refers to his time in Medina and indicates that he would observe this retreat either at its appointed time or by making it up later. It is authentically reported that he once intended to observe a retreat, and his wives asked to join him. Seeing that some of them were motivated by rivalry, he ordered the tents to be taken down and did not observe the retreat that year until he made it up in Shawwāl.

He, peace and blessings be upon him, fasted Ramadan only nine times, for fasting it was made obligatory in the second year after the Hijrah, after he had fasted the day of ‘Āshūrā’ and commanded the people to fast it on one occasion. He arrived in Medina in Rabī‘ al-Awwal of the first year, when ‘Āshūrā’ had already passed, so he did not command its observance that year. When the second year began, he commanded the people to fast it. Was this a command imposing an obligation, or

---

## Page 12744

merely a recommendation? There are two views on this among the scholars of our school and others. The correct view is that it was a binding command, issued during the day; they had not been commanded to fast from the preceding night. Later that year—in Rajab or another month—fasting the month of Ramadan was made obligatory. During Ramadan that year—the first Ramadan in which fasting was obligatory—the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, fought the Battle of Badr. It took place on Friday, the seventeenth of the month. After Allah granted him victory over the polytheists, he remained on the battlefield for three days. The last ten days thus began while he was travelling, and he returned to Medina with only a small part of those ten days remaining. He did not observe devotional retreat in Medina during that period, and he spent its remaining days attending to the captives and their ransom. After consulting his Companions about the ransom, he rose and entered his house, then came out again.

The reports of his activities indicate that he did not spend all of those ten days in devotional retreat. However, he may have made up his retreat, just as he made up his missed fasts and the retreat of the year in which his wives wanted to join him. That accounts for the year of Badr.

Likewise, in the year of the conquest, eight AH, he travelled during Ramadan and entered Mecca partway through the month, with only a small part of it remaining. While in Mecca, he was occupied with the aftermath of the conquest, dispatching military detachments to the surrounding areas, and establishing the foundations of

---

## Page 12745

Islam in Mecca, the Mother of Cities, and preparing to campaign against Hawāzin when he learned that they had assembled against him with Mālik ibn ‘Awf al-Nadrī. During the campaign of the conquest, he stayed in Mecca for nineteen nights, shortening his prayers.

They explained that, owing to the campaign against Hawāzin, he had not formed a definite intention to remain in Mecca. He was therefore a traveller there and was not free to devote himself to devotional retreat in Mecca that year. These, then, were three years in which he did not observe devotional retreat during Ramadan. He made up the retreat for the one year in which he had intended to observe it but then abandoned it. As for the other two years, Allah knows best whether he made them up, as with fasting, or did not make them up, as with the omitted half of the prayer.

It is authentically reported that he said:

“When a servant falls ill or travels, the deeds he used to perform while healthy and resident are recorded for him.”

It is also authentically reported that he said:

“Allah has relieved the traveller of fasting and half the prayer.”

That is, the traveller is exempt from fasting at its prescribed time, and from both performing half the prayer at its prescribed time and making it up later. Devotional retreat is analogous to one of these two. It has not been reported that he made up a devotional retreat missed while travelling, so the permissibility of doing so is not established. Nevertheless, in view of the general wording of ‘Ā’ishah’s hadith, the possibility remains. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 12746

He was asked:

Is it recommended to arrange a complete recitation of the Quran each year on the night of the birth of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Gathering people for food on the two Eids and the days of Tashrīq is a Sunnah. It is among the rites of Islam that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) instituted for the Muslims. Helping the poor by feeding them during Ramadan is also among the established practices of Islam. The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Whoever provides a fasting person with food to break his fast will have a reward like his.”

Giving poor Quran reciters the means to support their recitation is a righteous deed at any time. Whoever helps them in this shares in their reward.

As for instituting recurring observances other than those prescribed by Islamic law—such as certain nights of Rabīʿ al-Awwal said to mark the night of the Prophet’s birth, certain nights of Rajab, the eighteenth of Dhū al-Hijjah, the first Friday of Rajab, or the eighth of Shawwāl, which the ignorant call the “Festival of the Righteous”—these are innovations that the early generations neither regarded as recommended nor practised. Allah, glorified and exalted be He, knows best.

---

## Page 12747

Shaykh al-Islām was also asked:

Regarding what people do on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ—applying kohl, bathing, applying henna, shaking hands, cooking grains, displaying joy, and other such practices extending into the street—has any authentic hadith concerning this been reported from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), or not? If no authentic hadith has been reported concerning any of this, does practising it constitute an innovation or not? And do the practices of the other group—holding mourning gatherings, grieving, enduring thirst, lamenting, wailing, reciting accounts of the fallen, and tearing the neck openings of their garments—have any basis or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. No authentic report concerning any of this has been transmitted from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) or his Companions. Nor did any of the imams of the Muslims regard these practices as recommended, whether the four imams or anyone else. The compilers of the authoritative collections have not transmitted anything concerning this from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), the Companions, or the Successors, whether authentic or weak—neither in the collections of authentic hadith

---

## Page 12748

nor in the Sunan or Musnad collections. None of these reports was known during the virtuous early generations. Some later writers, however, transmitted reports on this subject, such as the claim that whoever applies kohl on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ will not suffer eye inflammation that year, and whoever bathes on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ will not fall ill that year, and similar claims.

They also transmitted reports about the merits of prayer on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ, and reports that this was the day of Adam’s repentance, the Ark’s coming to rest upon Mount al-Jūdī, Joseph’s return to Jacob, Abraham’s deliverance from the fire, and the ransoming of the son destined for sacrifice with a ram, and other such events. They also transmitted a fabricated report falsely attributed to the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace):

“Whoever spends generously on his household on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ, Allah will grant him abundance for the rest of the year.”

Attributing any of this to the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) is a lie. This saying is, however, known through a report transmitted by Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah from Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn al-Muntashir, from his father, who said:

“It has reached us that whoever spends generously on his household on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ, Allah will grant him abundance for the rest of his year.”

Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn al-Muntashir was from Kufa, and among the people of Kufa there were two groups:

---

## Page 12749

a group of Rāfidīs who outwardly professed allegiance to the Prophet’s household, while inwardly they were either irreligious heretics or ignorant people and followers of their own desires; and a group of Nāsibīs who hated ʿAlī and his companions because of the fighting that had taken place during the civil strife.

It is established in Sahīh Muslim that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Among Thaqīf there will arise a liar and a slaughterer.”

The liar was al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd al-Thaqafī. He outwardly professed allegiance to the Prophet’s household and support for their cause. He killed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād, the governor of Iraq who had dispatched the military detachment that killed al-Husayn ibn ʿAlī (may Allah be pleased with them both). Then he openly propagated falsehood, claiming prophethood and asserting that Gabriel (peace be upon him) descended to him. Matters reached the point that people spoke to Ibn ʿUmar and Ibn ʿAbbās about him.

They said to one of them, “Al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd claims that something is sent down to him.” He replied, “He speaks the truth. Allah Most High says:

‘Shall I inform you upon whom the devils descend? They descend upon every sinful liar.’”

They said to the other, “Al-Mukhtār claims that he receives revelation.” He replied, “He speaks the truth:

‘Indeed, the devils inspire their allies to argue with you.’”

As for the slaughterer, he was al-Hajjāj ibn Yūsuf al-Thaqafī, who was hostile towards ʿAlī and his companions. Thus, the latter belonged to the Nāsibīs and the former to the Rāfidīs; and this

---

## Page 12750

Rāfidī was far greater in lying, fabrication, and heresy in religion, for he claimed prophethood. The other, however, was harsher in punishing those who rebelled against his authority and in taking vengeance on anyone he suspected of disobeying his emir, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. There was strife and fighting between these two groups in Kufa.

When al-Husayn ibn ʿAlī (may Allah be pleased with them both) was killed on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ, it was the unjust, transgressing faction that killed him. Allah honored al-Husayn with martyrdom, just as He had honored others of his household with it: Hamza, Jaʿfar, his father ʿAlī, and others. His martyrdom was among the means by which Allah raised his standing and elevated his rank. For he and his brother al-Hasan are the two leaders of the young men of Paradise, and lofty ranks are attained only through trials. Thus, when the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) was asked, “Which people are most severely tried?” he replied:

“The prophets, then the righteous, then those nearest to them in excellence, followed by those next nearest. A man is tried according to his religious commitment. If his commitment is firm, his trials are increased; if it is weak, they are lightened. Trials continue to afflict the believer until he walks upon the earth without a single sin remaining upon him.”

This was narrated by al-Tirmidhī and others.

Allah Most High had already decreed a lofty station for al-Hasan and al-Husayn, yet they had not undergone trials like those endured by their righteous predecessors. They were born when Islam was strong and were raised in honor and dignity, with the Muslims venerating and honoring them. The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) died before they had fully attained the age of discernment.

---

## Page 12751

so Allah favored them by subjecting them to trials that would bring them to the rank of their household, just as He had tried those who were superior to them. For ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib was superior to them both, and he was killed as a martyr.

The killing of al-Husayn stirred up strife among the people, just as the killing of ʿUthmān (may Allah be pleased with him) was among the greatest causes of strife. Because of it, the Muslim community remains divided to this day. Hence the hadith states:

“Three things—whoever escapes them has truly escaped: my death, the killing of a persecuting caliph, and the Dajjāl.”

The death of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) was among the greatest causes of the trials through which many people fell into apostasy and abandoned Islam. Then Allah Most High raised up al-Siddīq (may Allah be pleased with him), through whom He strengthened faith and restored matters to their former state. He brought the apostates back through the door by which they had departed and kept the believers firm upon the religion they had entered. Allah endowed him with strength, striving in His cause, firmness toward His enemies, and gentleness toward His allies. These and his other qualities made him worthy of being the successor to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace).

He then appointed ʿUmar as his successor. ʿUmar subdued the unbelievers among the Magians and the People of the Book, strengthened Islam, founded cities, instituted stipends, established the administrative register, spread justice, and upheld the Sunnah. During his reign, Islam gained such ascendancy that the truth of Allah Most High’s words became manifest:

“He is the One Who sent His Messenger with guidance and the religion of truth to make it prevail over

---

## Page 12752

all other religions; and sufficient is Allah as a witness.” And His words, Most High:

“Allah has promised those of you who believe and do righteous deeds that He will surely grant them succession on earth, as He granted it to those before them; that He will surely establish for them their religion which He has chosen for them; and that He will surely replace their fear with security. They will worship Me, associating nothing with Me.”

And the words of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace):

“When Chosroes perishes, there will be no Chosroes after him, and when Caesar perishes, there will be no Caesar after him. By Him in Whose Hand is my soul, you will surely spend the treasures of them both in Allah’s cause.”

It was ʿUmar (may Allah be pleased with him) who spent their treasures. It is therefore clear that he spent them in Allah’s cause and that he was a rightly guided caliph, guided by Allah. He then entrusted the choice of his successor to a council of six. The Emigrants and Helpers agreed to appoint ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, without his offering them any inducement or intimidating them with any threat. They all pledged allegiance to him willingly and without reluctance.

Toward the end of his reign, circumstances arose in which the ignorant and aggressive gained the upper hand over the learned through evil deeds. They continued stirring up strife until the caliph was wrongfully killed as a martyr, without any grounds that would permit his killing. He remained patient, seeking Allah’s reward, and did not fight any Muslim.

When he was killed (may Allah be pleased with him), hearts became divided, distress intensified, evil people rose to prominence, and the good were humbled. Those who had previously been unable to foment strife now worked to do so, while those who wished to establish goodness and righteousness found themselves unable to do so. They then pledged allegiance to the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib (may Allah be pleased with him), who was then the person most entitled to the caliphate

---

## Page 12753

and the best of those who remained. Yet hearts were divided and the flames of strife were raging. No agreement was reached, the community was not united, and the caliph and the best members of the Muslim community were unable to accomplish all the good they intended. Some people became embroiled in division and strife, and events unfolded as they did, until the renegade Harūriyyah emerged, despite their abundant prayers, fasting, and recitation. They fought the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī, and those with him. He therefore killed them at the command of Allah and His Messenger, in obedience to the words of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), who described them as follows:

“Each of you would consider his prayer insignificant beside theirs, his fasting beside theirs, and his recitation beside theirs. They recite the Quran, but it does not go beyond their throats. They pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its quarry. Wherever you encounter them, kill them, for whoever kills them will have a reward with Allah on the Day of Resurrection.”

And his words:

“A faction will break away at a time of division among the Muslims. They will be killed by whichever of the two groups is nearer to the truth.”

It is recorded in the two Sahīh collections.

These Harūriyyah were thus the faction that broke away, while there was division among the believers. Fighting among believers does not take them outside the faith, as Allah Most High says:

“If two groups of believers fight, make peace between them. But if one of them transgresses against the other, fight the transgressing group until it returns to Allah’s command. If it returns, make peace between them with justice, and be equitable. Indeed, Allah loves those who act equitably.”

“The believers are but brothers, so make peace between your brothers.”

Thus He, glorified and exalted, made clear that, despite their fighting and the aggression of some of them

---

## Page 12754

against one another, they remained believers and brothers, and He commanded that peace be made between them. If either group then transgressed, the transgressing group was to be fought; He did not command fighting at the outset. The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) stated that the renegade faction would be killed by whichever of the two groups was nearer to the truth. It was ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib and those with him who fought them. The Prophet’s words thus indicated that they were nearer to the truth than Muʿāwiyah and those with him, while affirming the faith of both groups.

Then ʿAbd al-Rahmān ibn Muljam, one of these renegades, killed the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī, who passed as a martyr into Allah’s honour and good pleasure. The Companions pledged allegiance to his son al-Hasan, whose virtue then became manifest, just as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) had foretold in the authentic hadith:

“This son of mine is a leader, and through him Allah will reconcile two great groups of Muslims.”

He relinquished authority, and through him Allah reconciled the two groups. This was something for which the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) commended and praised him. It indicated that reconciliation between them was something Allah and His Messenger loved and praised.

He later died and passed into Allah’s honour and good pleasure. Groups then came forward and wrote to al-Husayn, promising him support and assistance if he assumed leadership, but they were not capable of

---

## Page 12755

doing so. Indeed, when he sent his cousin to them, they broke their promise and violated their pledge to him. They helped against him the very people whom they had promised to ward off and fight alongside him.

Those of sound judgment who loved al-Husayn, such as Ibn ‘Abbās, Ibn ‘Umar and others, advised him not to go to them or accept their invitation. They considered that his going to them would serve no good purpose and bring no welcome outcome. Events unfolded just as they had said, and Allah’s decree was already ordained.

When al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—set out and saw that circumstances had changed, he asked them to let him return, go to one of the frontier outposts, or join his cousin Yazīd. They denied him these options, insisting that he surrender as a captive. They fought him, and he fought them; then they killed him and a group of those accompanying him. He was wrongfully slain as a martyr—a martyrdom through which Allah honoured him and reunited him with the good and pure members of his household. Through it, Allah also disgraced those who wronged and attacked him. This gave rise to evil among the people.

An ignorant and unjust faction—either heretical and hypocritical, or misguided and wayward—then began professing allegiance to him and his household while making the day of ‘Āshūrā’ a day of mourning, sorrow and wailing. On that day they display the practices of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance: slapping their cheeks, tearing their collars and uttering the cries of that age.

---

## Page 12756

And what Allah and His Messenger have commanded when calamity first strikes is simply patience, seeking Allah’s reward, and affirming that we belong to Allah and to Him we shall return. As the Most High says:

“And give glad tidings to the patient.”

“Those who, when calamity strikes them, say, ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and to Him we shall return.’”

“Upon them are blessings and mercy from their Lord, and they are the rightly guided.”

In al-Sahīh, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is reported to have said:

“He is not one of us who slaps his cheeks, tears his collar and utters the cries of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance.”

He also said:

“I disavow the woman who wails, shaves her head or tears her clothes in mourning.”

And he said:

“If a woman who wails over the dead does not repent before her death, she will be raised on the Day of Resurrection wearing a garment of tar and a covering of scabies.”

In al-Musnad, Fātimah bint al-Husayn reports from her father, al-Husayn, that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whenever a man who has suffered a calamity remembers it—even long afterwards—and says anew, ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and to Him we shall return,’ Allah grants him a reward equal to his reward on the day the calamity struck.”

This is part of Allah’s generosity towards the believers. When the calamity that befell al-Husayn or anyone else is remembered long afterwards, the believer should repeat this affirmation, as Allah and His Messenger commanded, so that he may receive a reward equal to that of the afflicted person on the day the calamity struck. And if Allah Most High has commanded patience and seeking His reward in the immediate aftermath

---

## Page 12757

of a calamity, how much more so when a long time has passed!

Thus Satan has made it seem good to the misguided and wayward to turn the day of ‘Āshūrā’ into an occasion of mourning, with lamentation, wailing, the recitation of sorrowful poems, and the narration of accounts containing many lies. Even what is true in these accounts serves only to renew grief and partisan zeal, stir up hatred and warfare, and sow discord among Muslims. They use this as a means of reviling the earliest forerunners in Islam and multiplying lies and turmoil in the world. Among the various groups within Islam, none has been known to surpass this misguided and wayward sect in lying, fomenting discord, and helping the unbelievers against the Muslims. They are worse than the renegade Khārijites, concerning whom the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“They kill the Muslims and leave the idolaters alone.”

These people help the Jews, Christians, and polytheists against the Prophet’s household—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and the believers of his community. Thus they helped the polytheist Turks and Tatars perpetrate killing, enslavement, and the destruction of homes in Baghdad and elsewhere against the household of prophethood, the wellspring of the message—the descendants of al-‘Abbās and other members of the Prophet’s household—and the believers. No eloquent speaker could enumerate the evil these people commit and the harm they inflict upon the Muslims.

These people were opposed by others who were either Nawāsib, fanatically hostile to al-Husayn and the members of

---

## Page 12758

his household, or ignorant people who countered corruption with corruption, lies with lies, evil with evil, and religious innovation with religious innovation. They fabricated reports about expressions of joy and happiness on the day of ‘Āshūrā’, such as applying kohl, using dyes, spending more generously on one’s family, preparing food beyond the usual fare, and similar practices associated with festivals and celebrations. Thus these people make ‘Āshūrā’ a festive occasion of celebration and rejoicing, while the others make it an occasion of mourning, sorrow, and grief. Both groups are mistaken and depart from the Sunnah, although the latter have worse intentions, greater ignorance, and more flagrant injustice. Yet Allah commands justice and kindness. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever among you lives after me will see much disagreement. You must therefore adhere to my Sunnah and the Sunnah of the rightly guided caliphs after me. Hold fast to it and bite onto it with your molar teeth. Beware of newly introduced matters, for every religious innovation is misguidance.”

Neither the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—nor his rightly guided caliphs instituted any of these practices on the day of ‘Āshūrā’: neither expressions of sorrow and grief nor expressions of happiness and joy. Rather, when he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—arrived in Medina, he found the Jews fasting on the day of ‘Āshūrā’ and asked:

“What is this?”

They replied, “This is the day on which Allah saved Moses from drowning, so we fast on it.”

He said, “We have a greater right

---

## Page 12759

to Moses than you do.” So he fasted on that day and instructed others to fast on it. Quraysh also revered it during the pre-Islamic era. He instructed the people to fast for only one day: he arrived in Medina in the month of Rabī‘ al-Awwal, and the following year he fasted on ‘Āshūrā’ and instructed others to do so. Later that year, fasting during Ramadan was made obligatory, and the fast of ‘Āshūrā’ was abrogated.

The scholars differed over whether fasting on that day had been obligatory or recommended. There are two well-known opinions, the more correct of which is that it had been obligatory. Thereafter, those who fasted on it did so as a recommended act. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—did not instruct the people at large to fast on it. Rather, he would say:

“This is the day of ‘Āshūrā’, and I am fasting on it. Whoever wishes may fast.”

He also said:

“Fasting on the day of ‘Āshūrā’ expiates the sins of one year, and fasting on the day of ‘Arafah expiates the sins of two years.”

Toward the end of his life, when he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—learned that the Jews observed it as a festival, he said:

“If I live until next year, I shall certainly fast on the ninth.”

This was to differ from the Jews and not resemble them in observing it as a festival. Some of the Companions and scholars did not fast on it or consider fasting on it recommended; rather, they disapproved of singling it out for fasting, as has been reported from a group of people from Kufa. Other scholars considered fasting on it recommended.

---

## Page 12760

And the correct view is that whoever fasts on it is recommended to fast the ninth as well, for this was the Prophet’s final instruction—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—as expressed in his words:

“If I live until next year, I shall certainly fast the ninth along with the tenth.”

This is explicitly stated in some chains of transmission of the hadith. This, then, is what the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—prescribed.

As for other practices—such as preparing food out of the ordinary, whether grains or other foods; wearing new clothes; spending more generously; purchasing the year’s supplies on that day; performing a special act of worship, such as a prayer particular to it; slaughtering animals specifically for the occasion; saving meat from the sacrificial animals to cook with grains; applying kohl or dye; bathing; shaking hands; exchanging visits; visiting mosques and shrines; and similar practices—these are reprehensible innovations. Neither the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—nor his rightly guided caliphs prescribed them. Nor did any of the leading Muslim scholars recommend them: neither Mālik, al-Thawrī, al-Layth ibn Sa‘d, Abū Hanīfah, al-Awzā‘ī, al-Shāfi‘ī, Ahmad ibn Hanbal, Ishāq ibn Rāhawayh, nor other leading Muslim authorities and scholars like them.

Although some later followers of these imams would instruct people to observe some of these practices, narrate hadiths and reports concerning them, and say, “Some of these are authentic,” they are unquestionably mistaken and in error, in the judgment of those with

---

## Page 12761

knowledge of the true nature of things.

Harb al-Kirmānī said in his Questions:

Ahmad ibn Hanbal was asked about the hadith, “Whoever spends more generously on his family on the day of ‘Āshūrā’…” He did not consider it of any worth.

The strongest evidence they have is a report transmitted from Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn al-Muntashir, from his father, who said:

“We have heard that whoever spends more generously on his family on the day of ‘Āshūrā’, Allah will grant him abundance throughout the year.”

Sufyān ibn ‘Uyaynah said, “We have tried this for sixty years and found it to be true.”

Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad was from Kufa, and he did not mention from whom he had heard this or through whom it had reached him. Perhaps the person who said this was one of the people of innovation who hate ‘Alī and his companions and seek to counter the Rāfidah with lies—countering falsehood with falsehood and innovation with innovation.

As for Ibn ‘Uyaynah’s statement, it provides no proof. Allah, glorified be He, blessed him with provision, but nothing about Allah’s granting him that blessing indicates that its cause was spending more generously on the day of ‘Āshūrā’. Allah also granted abundance to those who were the best of creation among the Emigrants and Helpers, yet they did not deliberately single out the day of ‘Āshūrā’ for spending more generously on their families.

This is like the many people who make a vow for some need they wish to have fulfilled. Allah then fulfils their need, and they imagine that the vow was the cause. Yet it has been authentically established

---

## Page 12762

in an authentic report that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “forbade making vows and said, ‘It brings no good; it only extracts something from the miser.’” Whoever supposes that his need was fulfilled only because of his vow has falsely attributed something to Allah and His Messenger. People are commanded to obey Allah and His Messenger, to follow His religion and His path, and to adhere to His guidance and direction. They must thank Allah for the immense blessing He bestowed upon them by sending among them a Messenger from their own number, reciting His verses to them, purifying them, and teaching them the Book and wisdom. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in an authentic hadith:

“The best speech is the speech of Allah, and the best guidance is the guidance of Muhammad. The worst matters are those newly introduced, and every religious innovation is misguidance.”

People of knowledge and discernment agree that even if a man were to fly through the air or walk on water, he should not be followed unless he conforms to the commands of Allah and His Messenger. Whoever sees a man disclose hidden matters or produce extraordinary effects, then follows him in opposition to the Quran and Sunnah, is like the followers of the Dajjāl. For the Dajjāl says to the sky, “Rain!” and it rains; he says to the earth, “Bring forth vegetation!” and it does; and he says to a ruined place, “Bring forth your treasures!” and treasures of gold and silver come forth with him. He kills a man, then commands him to rise, and he rises. Yet despite all this, he is a disbeliever, accursed and an enemy of Allah. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Every prophet warned his people against the Dajjāl, and I warn you against him. He is blind in one eye

---

## Page 12763

but Allah is not blind in one eye. Between the Dajjāl’s eyes is written ‘disbeliever’—k f r—which every believer can read, whether literate or illiterate. Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

It is also established in an authentic hadith that he said:

“When any of you sits during prayer, let him seek refuge in Allah from four things, saying: ‘O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the punishment of Hell, from the punishment of the grave, from the trials of life and death, and from the trial of the false messiah, the Dajjāl.’”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said:

“The Hour will not come until thirty lying impostors appear, each claiming to be the Messenger of Allah.”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Before the Hour, there will be lying impostors who tell you things that neither you nor your forefathers have heard. Beware of them.”

The devils descend upon these people and inspire them, as Allah Most High says:

“Shall I inform you upon whom the devils descend? They descend upon every sinful liar. They pass on what they hear, and most of them are liars.”

Among the earliest of these people to appear was al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd, mentioned previously.

Whoever fails to distinguish between satanic states and states bestowed by the Most Merciful is like someone who equates Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah, with Musaylimah the Liar. Musaylimah had a devil who would descend upon him and inspire him.

---

## Page 12764

One sign of these people is that, when such states descend upon them while they listen to whistling and clapping, they froth at the mouth and tremble—like someone possessed—and utter words whose meaning cannot be understood. The devils speak through their tongues, just as they speak through the tongue of someone possessed.

The fundamental principle in this matter is to know that the friends of Allah are those whom Allah describes in His Book, saying:

“Indeed, the friends of Allah will have no fear, nor will they grieve—those who believe and fear Allah.”

Thus, everyone who believes and fears Allah is a friend of Allah.

In an authentic hadith, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘Whoever shows enmity to a friend of Mine has openly challenged Me to war. My servant draws near to Me through nothing like fulfilling what I have made obligatory upon him. My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him. When I love him, I am his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks. Through Me he hears, through Me he sees, through Me he grasps, and through Me he walks. If he asks Me, I will surely give him; and if he seeks My protection, I will surely protect him. I have never hesitated over anything I am going to do as I hesitate over taking the soul of My believing servant: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress, yet he must undergo it.’”

The religion of Islam is founded on two principles: that we worship none but Allah, and that

---

## Page 12765

we worship Him as He has prescribed, not through religious innovations.

Allah Most High says:

“So whoever hopes to meet his Lord, let him do righteous deeds and associate no one in the worship of his Lord.”

Righteous deeds are those that Allah and His Messenger love: deeds prescribed by Allah and in accordance with the Sunnah. This is why ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, used to say in his supplication:

“O Allah, make all my deeds righteous, make them sincerely for Your sake, and let no one else have any share in them.”

This is also why the foundations of Islam revolve around three hadiths: the Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Actions are judged only by intentions, and each person will have only what they intended.”

His saying:

“Whoever performs a deed that is not in accordance with our teaching will have it rejected.”

And his saying:

“What is lawful is clear, and what is unlawful is clear. Between them are doubtful matters that many people do not know. Whoever avoids doubtful matters safeguards his religion and honour, while whoever falls into doubtful matters falls into what is unlawful, like a shepherd grazing his flock around a protected pasture, on the verge of entering it. Indeed, every king has a protected domain, and Allah’s protected domain consists of the things He has forbidden. Indeed, within the body is a morsel of flesh: if it is sound, the whole body is sound, and if it is corrupt, the whole body is corrupt. Indeed, it is the heart.”

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. May Allah bless our master Muhammad, his family and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 12766

He was asked about the religious innovations associated with Thursday and similar occasions.

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, and may blessings and peace be upon Muhammad and his Companions. To proceed:

Satan has made certain practices seem appealing to many who profess Islam—practices they observe toward the end of the Christians’ fast, on that contemptible Thursday: giving gifts, holding festivities, spending money, clothing children, and other things that make it resemble a Muslim festival.

As for this Thursday at the end of the Christians’ fast, all the practices people introduce on it are reprehensible. These include women going out, burning incense at graves, laying clothes on rooftops, writing on sheets of paper and sticking them to doors, and making it an occasion for buying and selling intoxicating drinks. They also include reciting incantations over incense in any form, whether at that time or another, or deliberately seeking to buy incense over which incantations have been recited. Reciting incantations over incense and using it as a religious offering belong to the religion of the Christians and the Sabians. Incense is merely a fragrance whose smoke is used for perfuming, just as other fragrances are used. The same applies to singling out this day for cooking foods and other practices, such as dyeing eggs.

---

## Page 12767

As for gambling with eggs, selling them to those who gamble with them, or buying them from gamblers, the ruling is clear. These practices also include women taking olive leaves or washing with olive-leaf water, for this originates in baptismal water.

They also include suspending regular activities in crafts, trade, or study circles during their festivals, making the occasion a day of rest and rejoicing, and other such practices. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade them to observe the two days on which they used to amuse themselves in pre-Islamic times. He also forbade slaughtering animals in a place where the polytheists used to worship.
