They do things that make the believer’s heart shudder—a believer whose heart has not died, but who recognizes what is right and rejects what is wrong. Just as one must not imitate them, a Muslim who imitates them must not be helped to do so; rather, he must be told not to.

If someone holds a feast during their festivals that departs from his usual practice, his invitation should not be accepted. If a Muslim gives a gift during these festivals that departs from his usual practice at other times, his gift should not be accepted, especially if it helps people imitate them. Examples include giving candles and the like at Christmas, or giving eggs, milk, and sheep on “Little Thursday” at the end of their fast—that contemptible Thursday. Nor

---

## Page 12768

should a Muslim sell food, clothing, or incense that Muslims use to imitate them in their festivals, for this assists in wrongdoing.

The Shaykh—may Allah be pleased with him—said:

We shall mention some reprehensible practices of the Christian religion, because I have seen groups of Muslims afflicted with some of them. Many Muslims are unaware that these practices belong to the Christians’ religion, which is accursed, as are its adherents. I have been told that they go out to the graves on the contemptible Thursday preceding that, or on Saturday, or at other such times.

They also burn incense at these times, believing that it brings blessings and wards off harm. They regard it as an offering, like sacrificial animals. They perform incantations over it using a copper object which they strike like a small bell, together with composed formulas. They mark crosses on the doors of their homes and engage in other reprehensible practices. The markets even remain filled with the sounds of little bells and the recitations of astrologers and other incantation-reciters—words that are mostly false, some of which are forbidden or constitute unbelief.

It has been impressed upon the great majority of the common people—or all of them, except those whom Allah wills—and I mean

---

## Page 12769

by “the common people” here all those who do not know the true nature of Islam, since many reputed for religious learning and piety have joined them in this—that this incense over which incantations have been recited protects, through its blessing, against the evil eye, sorcery, ailments, and harm from the air. They make pictures of snakes and scorpions and affix them inside their homes, claiming that these pictures—whose makers are accursed, and whose presence prevents angels from entering a house—keep vermin away. This is a kind of Sabian talisman. Moreover, many of them, according to what has reached me, mark a cross on the door of the house.

Vast numbers go out on the earlier contemptible Thursday, and on this occasion they burn incense at graves. They call this later Thursday “Great Thursday,” whereas before Allah it is a lowly, contemptible Thursday—as are its adherents and those who venerate it. Anything falsely venerated, whether a place, a time, a stone, a tree, or a building, must deliberately be treated with contempt, just as worshipped idols are treated with contempt, even though, were they not worshipped, they would be like any other stones.

Among the reprehensible things people do is impose levies on peasants, mostly against their will, in the form of sheep, chickens, milk, and eggs. This combines two forbidden acts: wrongfully taking the property of Muslims and those under covenant, and maintaining a Christian religious observance. They make it an appointed time for sending agents out to the farms. They cook some of these provisions, dye eggs on that day, spend lavishly, and dress up their children

---

## Page 12770

and do other things that make the believer’s heart shudder—one whose heart has not died, but still recognizes good and rejects evil. Many of them leave their clothes out beneath the sky, hoping for the blessing of Mary’s descent upon them. Can anyone with even the smallest grain of faith in his heart doubt that the One who ordained a religious law requiring Muslims to differ from Jews and Christians—some examples of which we have already mentioned—would disapprove of even some of these abominations?

The root of all this is simply singling out the disbelievers’ festivals for some new practice, or imitating them in some of their observances. Thursday is their Feast of the Table, while Sunday they call Easter, the Feast of Light, and the Great Feast. Because it is a festival, they prepare dyed eggs and similar things for their children, since on that day they eat animal products such as meat, milk, and eggs; their fast consists of abstaining from animal flesh and animal products.

Satan has made most of these practices related about the Christians, as well as others not mentioned here, appealing to many who profess Islam. He has given these practices an esteemed place in their hearts and led them to regard them favorably. They have added to some of them and omitted others, brought some forward and postponed others. Whatever practices they or others reserve for these days, a Muslim may not imitate them, either in the practice itself or in the manner of performing it.

These practices also include covering their animals in red, coloring foods of a kind scarcely prepared even for the festival ordained by Allah and His Messenger, and exchanging gifts like those given during the pilgrimage seasons. Most of them have forgotten the origin of all this

---

## Page 12771

and it has persisted as an established custom. All this confirms the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“You will surely follow the ways of those who came before you.”

If following them even in small matters is a means of leading to some of these abominations, it is forbidden. What, then, if it leads to disbelief in Allah, such as seeking blessings through the cross, undergoing baptism, or saying, “The One worshipped is the same, even if the paths differ,” and similar statements and actions? These imply either that the Christian or Jewish religious law—both altered and abrogated—leads to Allah; approval of some of their teachings that contradict Allah’s religion; adopting such teachings as religious practice; or other things that constitute disbelief in Allah, His Messenger, the Quran, and Islam, without any disagreement within the Muslim community. The root of all this is resembling them and participating in their practices.

This makes clear to you the excellence of the upright monotheistic law, and some of the wisdom behind Allah’s prescribing for His Messenger that Muslims remain distinct from the disbelievers and differ from them in matters generally. Such distinctness more decisively cuts off evil at its source and keeps people further from falling into what others have fallen into.

Thus, if a Muslim’s family and children ask him for any of these things, he should direct them instead to what Allah and His Messenger have provided, and give them their due on Allah’s festival in a way that ends their longing for any other. If they are not satisfied, then there is no power and no strength except

---

## Page 12772

through Allah. Whoever displeases his family for Allah’s sake, Allah will bring contentment to him and to them.

Let the sensible man beware of obeying women in this matter. In the two Sahīh collections, Usāma ibn Zayd reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I have left behind me no trial more harmful to men than women.”

Nothing does more to corrupt kingship and states than obedience to women. In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Abū Bakra reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A people who entrust their affairs to a woman will not prosper.”

It is also reported:

“Men perished when they obeyed women.”

When the Mothers of the Believers questioned his decision to put Abū Bakr forward, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to them:

“You are like the women in Joseph’s story.”

He meant that it is characteristic of women to argue with a man of sound judgment, as he said in another hadith:

“I have never seen anyone deficient in intellect and religion more capable of overcoming a discerning man’s judgment than one of you.”

When al-A‘shā—al-A‘shā of Bāhila—recited to him his verses containing the line:

“They are the worst conquerors for those they conquer,”

the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, began repeating it, saying:

“They are the worst conquerors for those they conquer.”

For this reason, Allah, glorified is He, mentioned His favor to Zechariah, saying:

“We set his wife right for him.”

Some scholars said that a man should earnestly beseech Allah to set his wife right.

---

## Page 12773

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said:

“Whoever imitates a people is one of them.”

Al-Bayhaqī narrated with an authentic chain of transmission, in the chapter on the disapproval of entering the polytheists’ churches while they are there on their festival day, and of imitating them on their Nayrūz and Mihrjān, from Sufyān al-Thawrī, from Thawr ibn Yazīd, from ‘Atā’ ibn Dīnār, who said that ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, said:

“Do not learn the language of non-Arabs, and do not enter the polytheists’ churches while they are there on their festival day, for divine wrath descends upon them.”

Here ‘Umar forbade learning their language and even merely entering their church while they are there on their festival day. What, then, of someone who performs some of their deeds or deliberately pursues something entailed by their religion? Is not conformity with them in deeds more serious than conformity with them in language? Is not performing some of the acts associated with their festival more serious than merely entering their gathering during their festival? If divine wrath descends upon them on their festival day because of their deeds, has not someone who shares in those deeds, or some of them, exposed himself to punishment for that?

Furthermore, does his statement,

“Avoid the enemies of Allah during their festival,”

not prohibit meeting them and gathering with them on that occasion? What, then, of someone who celebrates their festival?

Ibn ‘Umar said in the course of a statement:

“Whoever observes their Nayrūz and Mihrjān and imitates them until he dies will be gathered with them.”

‘Umar said:

“Avoid the enemies of Allah during their festival.”

Imam Ahmad explicitly stated that

---

## Page 12774

it is not permissible to attend the festivals of the Jews and Christians. He cited as evidence Allah’s words:

“And those who do not bear witness to falsehood.”

He explained: “Sha‘ānīn and their festivals.”

‘Abd al-Malik ibn Habīb, one of Mālik’s followers, said in the course of a discussion:

“They must not be assisted in anything connected with their festival, because that would honour their polytheism and assist them in their disbelief. Rulers should prohibit Muslims from doing so. This is the position of Mālik and others; I know of no disagreement concerning it.

Eating animals slaughtered for their festivals falls within this unanimously disapproved conduct; indeed, in my view, it is more serious. Abū al-Qāsim was asked about travelling aboard ships carrying Christians to their festivals. He disapproved of this, fearing that divine wrath might descend upon them because of the polytheism for which they had assembled. Allah Most High has said:

‘O you who believe, do not take the Jews and Christians as allies. They are allies of one another. Whoever among you takes them as allies’—thus agreeing with them and assisting them—‘is indeed one of them.’”

Imam Ahmad narrated with an authentic chain of transmission that Abū Mūsā said:

“I said to ‘Umar, ‘I have a Christian secretary.’ He said, ‘What is wrong with you? May Allah strike you down! Have you not heard Allah Most High say, “O you who believe, do not take the Jews and Christians as allies. They are allies of one another”? Why did you not employ a Muslim instead?’ I replied, ‘Commander of the Faithful, I have

---

## Page 12775

his secretarial services, while his religion is his own.’ He said, ‘I do not honour them when Allah has dishonoured them, nor exalt them when Allah has humbled them, nor bring them close when Allah has cast them far away.’”

Allah Most High says:

“And those who do not bear witness to falsehood.”

Mujāhid explained this as “the festivals of the polytheists,” as did al-Rabī‘ ibn Anas. Al-Qādī Abū Ya‘lā said:

(A discussion of the prohibition of attending the festivals of the polytheists.

Abū al-Shaykh al-Asbahānī narrated, with his chain of transmission, in his work on the conditions governing protected non-Muslims, that al-Dahhāk explained Allah’s words, “And those who do not bear witness to falsehood,” as “the festival of the polytheists.” He also narrated, through Sinān, that al-Dahhāk explained “And those who do not bear witness to falsehood” as referring to “the speech of the polytheists.”

He further narrated, with his chain of transmission, from Ibn Salām, from ‘Amr ibn Murrah, concerning “And those who do not bear witness to falsehood”: “They do not remain with the polytheists in their polytheism, nor mingle with them.”

The Book, the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and the Sunnah of his rightly guided caliphs, upon which the scholars have agreed, establish that Muslims should differ from them and refrain from imitating them. . . . (1)

Lighting fires and rejoicing in them are among the distinctive practices of the Magians, the worshippers of fire. A Muslim strives to revive the practices of the Sunnah and eradicate religious innovations. For the two Sahīh collections report from Abū Hurayrah—may Allah be pleased with him—that the Messenger of Allah said:

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) A blank space in the original.

---

## Page 12776

—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—

“The Jews and Christians do not dye their hair, so differ from them.”

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—also said:

“The Jews have incurred Allah’s wrath, and the Christians have gone astray.”

Allah Most High has commanded us to say in our prayers:

“Guide us to the straight path—the path of those You have blessed, not of those who have incurred Your wrath, nor of those who have gone astray.”

And Allah, glorified be He, knows best.

---

## Page 12777

He was asked:

About Muslims who prepare food like that of the Christians at Nayrūz and observe the other festivals, such as Epiphany, Christmas, Lentil Thursday, and the Saturday of Light, and about those who sell Christians things they use for their festivals: is it permissible for Muslims to do any of this or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. It is not lawful for Muslims to imitate them in anything distinctive to their festivals, whether food, clothing, bathing, lighting fires, or suspending customary activities relating to livelihood, worship, or anything else. Nor is it lawful to hold a feast, give gifts, or sell things that assist in these observances for that purpose. Nor may children and the like be allowed to engage in the games associated with these festivals, nor may adornments be displayed.

In short, Muslims must not mark their festivals with any of their distinctive observances. Rather, their festival days should be like any other days for Muslims, who must not single them out for any practices distinctive to them.

---

## Page 12778

As for Muslims deliberately choosing that day, groups of earlier and later scholars have disapproved of this. As for singling it out for the practices mentioned above, the scholars agree that this is forbidden. Indeed, some scholars have held that whoever does these things commits disbelief, because they involve venerating the rites of disbelief.

Some of them said:

“Whoever slaughters a gored sheep on their festival day is as though he had slaughtered a pig.”

ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀs said:

“Whoever [imitates] (*) the people of non-Arab lands, celebrates their Nayrūz and Mahrajān, and continues imitating them until he dies in that state will be gathered with them on the Day of Resurrection.”

In Sunan Abī Dāwūd, Thābit ibn al-Dahhāk reported:

“A man vowed during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to slaughter camels at Buwānah. He came to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said, ‘I have vowed to slaughter camels at Buwānah.’ The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked, ‘Was there an idol there, among the idols of the pre-Islamic age, that was worshipped besides Allah?’

“He replied, ‘No.’ He asked, ‘Was any of their festivals held there?’

“He replied, ‘No.’ The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘Fulfil your vow, for no vow is to be fulfilled in disobedience to Allah, nor concerning something a person does not own.’”

Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not permit this man to fulfil his vow—even though fulfilling a vow is obligatory in principle—until the man informed him that no festival of the disbelievers had been held there. He said, “No vow is to be fulfilled in

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 266): “The well-known reading is banā (built).”

---

## Page 12779

disobedience to Allah.” Thus, if slaughtering at a place where their festival was held is an act of disobedience, what then of joining them in the festival itself? Indeed, the Commander of the Faithful, Umar ibn al-Khattāb, the Companions, and the other imams of the Muslims stipulated that they must not celebrate their festivals publicly in Muslim lands, but only privately in their homes. What then if Muslims themselves celebrate them publicly? Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, even said:

“Do not learn the languages of the non-Arabs, and do not enter the polytheists’ churches on their festival day, for divine displeasure descends upon them.”

If someone who enters to watch, or for some other purpose, is forbidden to do so because divine displeasure descends upon them, what then of someone who performs the very practices that bring Allah’s displeasure upon them and form part of their religious rites?

More than one of the early Muslims, commenting on the words of Allah Most High,

“And those who do not bear witness to falsehood,”

said that this refers to the festivals of the disbelievers. If this applies to attending them without taking part, what then of performing the practices distinctive to them?

It is reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the Musnad and the Sunan collections that he said:

“Whoever imitates a people is one of them.”

Another wording states:

“Whoever imitates those other than us is not one of us.”

This is a good hadith. So if this is the case with imitating them, even if it is

---

## Page 12780

a matter of ordinary custom, what then of imitating them in something far more serious?

The majority of imams disapproved of eating what they slaughter for their festivals and sacrificial offerings—whether regarding it as forbidden or merely discouraged—classifying it among that over which a name other than Allah’s has been invoked and that which is slaughtered on sacrificial altars. Likewise, Muslims were forbidden to assist them in their festivals through gifts or trade. They stated that it is not lawful for Muslims to sell Christians anything that serves their festival—not meat, blood, or clothing. Nor should they be lent a riding animal or assisted in any aspect of their religion, for this glorifies their polytheism and assists them in their disbelief. Rulers should forbid Muslims from doing this, because Allah Most High says:

“Cooperate in righteousness and piety, but do not cooperate in sin and aggression.”

Moreover, a Muslim is not permitted to help them drink wine by pressing grapes for it or by similar means. How, then, could he assist them in practices that are among the rites of disbelief? And if he is not permitted to assist them, what then if he performs those practices himself?

And Allah knows best. These are the words of Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah.
